Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

A boy named Ayrat lives on planet AMI-1511. Each inhabitant of this planet has a talent. Specifically, Ayrat loves running, moreover, just running is not enough for him. He is dreaming of making running a real art.

First, he wants to construct the running track with coating t. On planet AMI-1511 the coating of the track is the sequence of colored blocks, where each block is denoted as the small English letter. Therefore, every coating can be treated as a string.

Unfortunately, blocks aren't freely sold to non-business customers, but Ayrat found an infinite number of coatings s. Also, he has scissors and glue. Ayrat is going to buy some coatings s, then cut out from each of them exactly one continuous piece (substring) and glue it to the end of his track coating. Moreover, he may choose to flip this block before glueing it. Ayrat want's to know the minimum number of coating s he needs to buy in order to get the coating t for his running track. Of course, he also want's to know some way to achieve the answer.


-----Input-----

First line of the input contains the string s — the coating that is present in the shop. Second line contains the string t — the coating Ayrat wants to obtain. Both strings are non-empty, consist of only small English letters and their length doesn't exceed 2100.


-----Output-----

The first line should contain the minimum needed number of coatings n or -1 if it's impossible to create the desired coating.

If the answer is not -1, then the following n lines should contain two integers x_{i} and y_{i} — numbers of ending blocks in the corresponding piece. If x_{i} ≤ y_{i} then this piece is used in the regular order, and if x_{i} > y_{i} piece is used in the reversed order. Print the pieces in the order they should be glued to get the string t.


-----Examples-----
Input
abc
cbaabc

Output
2
3 1
1 3

Input
aaabrytaaa
ayrat

Output
3
1 1
6 5
8 7

Input
ami
no

Output
-1



-----Note-----

In the first sample string "cbaabc" = "cba" + "abc".

In the second sample: "ayrat" = "a" + "yr" + "at".

The input will be stdin and you should print your solution to stdout

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have an infinite supply of a coating string `s`
2. We want to build a target string `t` by:
   - Cutting out continuous substrings from `s` (each substring is a piece)
   - Optionally flipping (reversing) each piece before gluing
   - Gluing pieces to the end of our track
3. We need to find the minimum number of pieces needed, or -1 if impossible

## Key Insights

1. If we can't build `t` from `s`, return -1
2. Each piece we take from `s` can be either in original order or reversed order
3. We want to minimize the number of pieces
4. We need to find the optimal way to partition `t` into segments that can be formed from `s`

## Algorithm Approach

1. **Impossibility check**: If `t` contains a character not in `s`, it's impossible
2. **Greedy approach**: Try to build `t` by finding the longest possible prefix of `t` that can be formed from `s`
3. For each position, we try to find a substring in `s` that matches a prefix of the remaining part of `t`, either directly or in reverse

## Detailed Strategy

1. First, check if any character in `t` is not in `s`. If yes, return -1.
2. Use dynamic programming or greedy to find the minimum number of pieces.
3. At each step, for the remaining prefix of `t`, try to find a substring in `s` that matches it (in original or reversed order).
4. We want to maximize the length of each piece to minimize the total count.

## Implementation

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    t = input().strip()
    
    # Check if any character in t is not in s
    s_set = set(s)
    for char in t:
        if char not in s_set:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    # Find all possible substrings of s and their reverses
    # For efficiency, we'll use a dictionary to store positions of substrings
    
    # We'll use greedy approach: try to match as long as possible
    pieces = []
    remaining = t
    
    while remaining:
        # Try to find the longest substring of remaining that appears in s or reversed
        found = False
        max_len = 0
        best_start = -1
        best_end = -1
        
        # Try all possible lengths from current length down to 1
        for length in range(len(remaining), 0, -1):
            substring = remaining[:length]
            
            # Check if substring appears in s (original)
            if substring in s:
                # We found a match in original order
                pieces.append((0, length - 1))  # 0 means original order
                remaining = remaining[length:]
                found = True
                break
                
            # Check if reversed substring appears in s (reversed order)
            reversed_sub = substring[::-1]
            if reversed_sub in s:
                pieces.append((1, length - 1))  # 1 means reversed order
                remaining = remaining[length:]
                found = True
                break
        
        if not found:
            # If no substring found, check if we can form the rest with single characters
            if remaining:
                # Try all possible substrings of length 1
                found = False
                for i, char in enumerate(remaining):
                    if char in s:
                        pieces.append((0, 0))  # single character in original order
                        remaining = remaining[1:]
                        found = True
                        break
                if not found:
                    print(-1)
                    return
    
    # Output the result
    print(len(pieces))
    for piece in pieces:
        if piece[0] == 0:
            print(f"1 1")  # This doesn't quite match the expected format
        else:
            print(f"1 1")  # This doesn't work either
    
    # Let me rethink the output format
    # The problem says: "numbers of ending blocks in the corresponding piece"
    # If x <= y: original order, if x > y: reversed order
    # We need to output the start and end indices (1-indexed)
    
    # Actually, let me redo this more carefully

def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    t = input().strip()
    
    # Check if any character in t is not in s
    s_set = set(s)
    for char in t:
        if char not in s_set:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    pieces = []
    remaining = t
    
    while remaining:
        # Try to match the longest possible substring
        found = False
        for length in range(len(remaining), 0, -1):
            substring = remaining[:length]
            
            # Check if substring appears in s (original)
            if substring in s:
                # Original order
                start_idx = 1
                end_idx = length
                pieces.append((start_idx, end_idx))
                remaining = remaining[length:]
                found = True
                break
                
            # Check if reversed substring appears in s
            reversed_sub = substring[::-1]
            if reversed_sub in s:
                # Reversed order
                start_idx = length + 1
                end_idx = 1
                pieces.append((start_idx, end_idx))
                remaining = remaining[length:]
                found = True
                break
        
        if not found:
            # If no match found for length >= 1, try single characters
            char = remaining[0]
            if char in s:
                pieces.append((1, 1))
                remaining = remaining[1:]
            else:
                print(-1)
                return
    
    print(len(pieces))
    for start, end in pieces:
        print(start, end)
```

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    t = input().strip()
    
    # Check if any character in t is not in s
    s_set = set(s)
    for char in t:
        if char not in s_set:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    pieces = []
    remaining = t
    
    while remaining:
        found = False
        # Try from longest possible to shortest
        for length in range(len(remaining), 0, -1):
            substring = remaining[:length]
            
            # Check original order
            if substring in s:
                # Use original order: start=1, end=length (1-indexed)
                pieces.append((1, length))
                remaining = remaining[length:]
                found = True
                break
                
            # Check reversed order
            reversed_sub = substring[::-1]
            if reversed_sub in s:
                # Use reversed order: start=length+1, end=1 (since x>y for reversed)
                pieces.append((length + 1, 1))
                remaining = remaining[length:]
                found = True
                break
        
        if not found:
            # If no match found, try single characters
            char = remaining[0]
            if char in s:
                pieces.append((1, 1))
                remaining = remaining[1:]
            else:
                print(-1)
                return
```

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have an infinite supply of a string `s` and want to create target string `t`
2. From each copy of `s`, we can cut out a continuous substring and either use it in original order or reversed order
3. We need to find the minimum number of such substrings needed to form `t`
4. If impossible, return -1

## Key Insights

1. We need to check if `t` can be formed using substrings from `s` (either original or reversed)
2. For each character in `t`, we need to find which substring from `s` can produce it
3. Since we can use any substring from `s` (in original or reversed order), we need to check if each character in `t` appears in `s` (in some position or in reversed form)

## Algorithm Steps

1. First, check if it's possible: for each character in `t`, it must appear in `s` or in the reverse of `s`
2. If impossible, return -1
3. Greedily build the target string `t` by finding the shortest possible substrings from `s` that match the required segments

## Detailed Approach

1. For each position in `t`, we need to find a substring of `s` that can produce the current character(s)
2. We can use dynamic programming or greedy approach to build the result
3. Since we can use any substring (in any order and orientation), we need to track which parts of `s` we can use

## Step-by-step Solution

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    t = input().strip()
    
    # Check if each character in t exists in s or in reverse(s)
    s_rev = s[::-1]
    s_set = set(s)
    s_rev_set = set(s_rev)
    
    # Check if all characters in t exist in s or in reverse(s)
    for char in t:
        if char not in s_set and char not in s_rev_set:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    # Build the result using greedy approach
    # We'll try to match t with substrings from s and s_rev
    
    result = []
    i = 0  # current position in t
    
    while i < len(t):
        # Try to find a substring from s that matches the remaining part of t
        found = False
        
        # Try to match with original s
        j = 0
        while j < len(s):
            # Check if we can match the required substring from s starting at position j
            match_len = 0
            # Find longest prefix of remaining t that matches s[j:] or s_rev[j:]
            if j + match_len < len(s):
                while (j + match_len < len(s) and 
                       i + match_len < len(t) and 
                       s[j + match_len] == t[i + match_len]):
                    match_len += 1
            
            # Check if we can match with reversed s
            if j + match_len < len(s):
                # Check reverse match
                rev_match_len = 0
                while (j + rev_match_len < len(s) and 
                       i + match_len + rev_match_len < len(t) and 
                       s_rev[j + rev_match_len] == t[i + match_len + rev_match_len]):
                    rev_match_len += 1
            
            # Try each possible substring length
            for length in range(1, len(t) - i + 1):
                # Try matching with original s
                if (j + length <= len(s) and 
                    all(s[j + k] == t[i + k] for k in range(length))):
                    result.append((j + 1, j + length))  # 1-indexed
                    i += length
                    found = True
                    break
                
                # Try matching with reversed s
                if (j + length <= len(s) and 
                    all(s[len(s) - 1 - (j + k)] == t[i + k] for k in range(length))):
                    result.append((j + 1, j + 1 - length))  # 1-indexed, reversed
                    i += length
                    found = True
                    break
            
            if found:
                break
        
        if not found:
            # Try to match with short segments
            # Find any character in t that matches s or s_rev
            char_found = False
            for pos in range(len(s)):
                if t[i] == s[pos] or t[i] == s_rev[pos]:
                    if t[i] == s[pos]:
                        result.append((pos + 1, pos + 1))
                    else:
                        result.append((pos + 1, pos + 1))
                    i += 1
                    char_found = True
                    break
            
            if not char_found:
                print(-1)
                return
    
    # Print result
    print(len(result))
    for start, end in result:
        if start <= end:
            print(start, end)
        else:
            print(start, end)

solve()
```

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    t = input().strip()
    
    # Check if it's possible
    s_rev = s[::-1]
    s_chars = set(s)
    s_rev_chars = set(s_rev)
    
    for char in t:
        if char not in s_chars and char not in s_rev_chars:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    # Build the result greedily
    result = []
    i = 0
    
    while i < len(t):
        # Try to find a substring of s (original or reversed) that matches t[i:]
        found = False
        
        # First, try to find a single character match
        char_match = False
        for j in range(len(s)):
            if t[i] == s[j] or t[i] == s_rev[j]:
                if t[i] == s[j]:
                    result.append((j + 1, j + 1))
                else:
                    result.append((j + 1, j + 1))
                i += 1
                found = True
                char_match = True
                break
        
        if not found:
            # Try longer substrings
            for length in range(1, len(t) - i + 1):
                can_match_original = True
                can_match_reversed = True
                
                # Check if we can match this length with original s
                for k in range(length):
                    if i + k >= len(t):
                        break
                    if j + k >= len(s):
                        can_match_original = False
                        break
                    if s[j + k] != t[i + k]:
                        can_match_original = False
                        break
                
                # Check if we can match this length with reversed s
                for k in range(length):
                    if i + k >= len(t):
                        break
                    if j + k >= len(s):
                        can_match_reversed = False
                        break
                    if s[len(s) - 1 - (j + k)] != t[i + k]:
                        can_match_reversed = False
                        break
                
                if can_match_original:
                    # Find the best starting position
                    for j in range(len(s) - length + 1):
                        match = True
                        for k in range(length):
                            if s[j + k] != t[i + k]:
                                match = False
                                break
                        if match:
                            result.append((j + 1, j + length))
                            i += length
                            found = True
                            break
                    if found:
                        break
                
                if can_match_reversed:
                    # Find the best starting position
                    for j in range(len(s) - length + 1):
                        match = True
                        for k in range(length):
                            if s[len(s) - 1 - (j + k)] != t[i + k]:
                                match = False
                                break
                        if match:
                            result.append((j + 1, j + 1 - length))
                            i += length
                            found = True
                            break
                    if found:
                        break
            
            if not found:
                print(-1)
                return
    
    print(len(result))
    for start, end in result:
        print(start, end)
```

## Problem Understanding

1. We have an infinite supply of a coating string `s`
2. We want to create target string `t` by cutting out substrings from `s` and either using them as-is or reversed
3. We need to find the minimum number of such substrings needed
4. For each substring, we need to output the start and end indices (1-indexed)

## Key Insights

1. If we can't create `t` from `s`, return -1
2. We need to find the minimum number of substrings from `s` that can form `t`
3. Each substring can be used in forward or reverse order
4. For each position in `t`, we need to find a substring in `s` that matches either the original or reversed form

## Algorithm Approach

2. We need to greedily match characters in `t` with substrings from `s`
3. For each position in `t`, find the shortest substring from `s` that can produce the required character(s)

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    t = input().strip()
    
    # Check if all characters in t are present in s
    s_chars = set(s)
    t_chars = set(t)
    if not t_chars.issubset(s_chars):
        print(-1)
        return
    
    n = len(t)
    m = len(s)
    
    # Find all substrings of s (to check if we can form any part of t)
    # For each possible substring in s, we can use it forward or backward
    # We want to find minimum number of substrings needed
    
    # DP where dp[i] = minimum number of pieces to form t[0:i]
    # and we store the pieces as well
    
    # First, build a set of all possible substrings and their reverses
    # For efficiency, we'll just store mappings from pattern to (start, end)
    
    # For each position in t, we'll try to find a substring from s
    # that matches t[i:j] or reverse(t[i:j])
    
    # Let's use DP: dp[i] = (min_pieces, pieces_list)
    dp = [float('inf')] * (n + 1)
    dp[0] = 0
    
    # To store the actual pieces
    pieces = [[] for _ in range(n + 1)]
    
    # For each position in t
    for i in range(n):
        # Try to extend from position i
        for j in range(i + 1, n + 1):
            substr_t = t[i:j]
            # Check if substr_t or reverse(substr_t) appears in s
            substr_t_rev = substr_t[::-1]
            
            # Check if substr_t appears in s
            found = False
            for start in range(m - len(substr_t) + 1):
                if s[start:start + len(substr_t)] == substr_t:
                    found = True
                    break
            
            # Check if substr_t_rev appears in s
            if not found:
                for start in range(m - len(substr_t_rev) + 1):
                    if s[start:start + len(substr_t_rev)] == substr_t_rev:
                        found = True
                        break
            
            if found:
                # We can form t[i:j] using one piece
                if dp[i] != float('inf'):
                    if dp[i] + 1 < dp[j]:
                        dp[j] = dp[i] + 1
                        pieces[j] = pieces[i] + [[i+1, j-1]] if i == 0 else pieces[i] + [[i+1, j-1]]
                else:
                    # We can form t[i] directly
                    if j == i + 1:
                        # This is just one character
                        pass
    
    # Actually, let me simplify - for each position, we need to find the best piece
    # We'll use a different approach: for each position, try to find the longest match
    
    # Let's try a simpler greedy approach
    # For each position, find the longest substring from s that matches the remaining part of t
    
    result_pieces = []
    remaining = list(t)
    pos = 0
    
    while pos < len(remaining):
        # Try to match as much as possible from current position
        max_len = 0
        best_end = pos
        
        # Try each possible length from 1 to remaining
        for length in range(1, len(remaining) - pos + 1):
            substr = ''.join(remaining[pos:pos + length])
            substr_rev = substr[::-1]
            
            # Check if substr appears in s
            found_substr = False
            for start in range(len(s) - length + 1):
                if s[start:start + length] == substr:
                    found_substr = True
                    break
            
            # Check if substr_rev appears in s
            found_rev = False
            for start in range(len(s) - length + 1):
                if s[start:start + length] == substr_rev:
                    found_rev = True
                    break
            
            if found_substr or found_rev:
                max_len = length
                best_end = pos + length
                if found_substr:
                    # Use forward
                    # Find the actual indices in s
                    for start in range(len(s) - length + 1):
                        if s[start:start + length] == substr:
                            result_pieces.append([start + 1, start + length])
                            break
                else:
                    # Use reverse
                    for start in range(len(s) - length + 1):
                        if s[start:start + length] == substr_rev:
                            result_pieces.append([start + 1, start + length])
                            break
                    # In reverse, the piece is from start to start+length, but used in reverse
                    # So the output should be (start+1, start+length) with x > y meaning reverse
                    # But we need to output the actual positions in s
                    break
            else:
                break
        
        if max_len == 0:
            print(-1)
            return
            
        # Move to next position
        pos = best_end
    
    # Actually, I think my approach is getting too complex
    # Let me go back to the DP approach that's more systematic
    
    # Initialize DP
    dp = [float('inf')] * (n + 1)
    dp[0] = 0
    parent = [-1] * (n + 1)  # To store the starting position of the last piece
    
    # For each position, try all possible previous positions
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        for j in range(i):
            # We want to go from position j to i
            substr = t[j:i]
            substr_rev = substr[::-1]
            
            # Check if substr or substr_rev appears in s
            found = False
            
            # Check if substr appears in s
            for start in range(len(s) - len(substr) + 1):
                if s[start:start + len(substr)] == substr:
                    found = True
                    break
            
            if not found:
                # Check if substr_rev appears in s
                for start in range(len(s) - len(substr_rev) + 1):
                    if s[start:start + len(substr_rev)] == substr_rev:
                        found = True
                        break
            
            if found:
                if dp[j] + 1 < dp[i]:
                    dp[i] = dp[j] + 1
                    parent[i] = j
    
    if dp[n] == float('inf'):
        print(-1)
        return
    
    # Reconstruct the pieces
    pieces = []
    pos = n
    while pos > 0:
        prev = parent[pos]
        substr = t[prev:pos]
        substr_rev = substr[::-1]
        
        # Find the actual indices in s that match substr or substr_rev
        found = False
        for start in range(len(s) - len(substr) + 1):
            if s[start:start + len(substr)] == substr:
                pieces.append([start + 1, start + len(substr)])
                found = True
                break
        
        if not found:
            for start in range(len(s) - len(substr_rev) + 1):
                if s[start:start + len(substr_rev)] == substr_rev:
                    # This means we used the reverse
                    pieces.append([start + 1, start + len(substr)])
                    found = True
                    break
        
        if not found:
            print(-1)
            return
            
        pos = prev
    
    # Reverse pieces since we built them in reverse order
    pieces.reverse()
    
    print(dp[n])
    for piece in pieces:
        print(piece[0], piece[1])
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have an infinite supply of a coating string `s`
2. We can cut out any substring from `s` and either use it as-is or in reverse order
3. We want to build target string `t` by concatenating these substrings (in some order)
4. We need to find the minimum number of such substrings needed

## Key Insights

1. Since we can take any substring from `s` and use it in forward or reverse order, we need to check if `t` can be constructed from pieces that are substrings of `s` (or their reverses).

2. For each position in `t`, we need to find a substring of `s` that matches the required pattern (original or reversed).

3. We can use a greedy approach: build `t` character by character, and at each step, find the shortest substring of `s` that can match the remaining part of `t` (either forward or backward).

4. However, we need to be careful about the order - we might need to use longer substrings that match multiple parts of `t`.

## Algorithm

1. First, check if it's impossible: if `t` contains a character not present in `s`, return -1.

2. Use dynamic programming or greedy approach to build `t`:
   - For each prefix of `t`, try to match it with a substring of `s` (in original or reversed form)
   - We want to minimize the number of pieces

3. We can use a greedy approach: at each step, find the longest possible match that can extend our current prefix.

4. For each position in `t`, we need to find a substring of `s` that matches the remaining suffix of `t` in either original or reversed order.

## Implementation Strategy

1. Check if any character in `t` is missing from `s` → return -1
2. Use a greedy approach: build `t` piece by piece
3. For each segment of `t`, try to find the shortest substring of `s` that can match it (in forward or backward)
4. For each candidate segment, check if it appears in `s` or in reverse

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    t = input().strip()
    
    # Check if it's impossible - any character in t not in s
    s_set = set(s)
    for char in t:
        if char not in s_set:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    # Find all substrings of s and their reverses
    # We'll use a dictionary to store (substring, positions) for efficiency
    # But since t can be up to 2100 chars, we need a smart approach
    
    # Greedy approach: build t piece by piece
    result = []
    remaining = t
    
    while remaining:
        # Try to find the shortest possible substring that matches remaining
        found = False
        
        # First check if remaining is already a substring of s
        for i in range(len(s)):
            for j in range(i, len(s)):
                substr = s[i:j+1]
                if substr == remaining:
                    result.append((i+1, j+1))  # 1-indexed
                    remaining = ""
                    found = True
                    break
            if found:
                break
                
        if found:
            break
            
        # Try if remaining is a reverse of a substring of s
        rev_remaining = remaining[::-1]
        for i in range(len(s)):
            for j in range(i, len(s)):
                substr = s[i:j+1]
                if substr == rev_remaining:
                    result.append((i+1, j+1))  # 1-indexed, but in reversed order
                    # Actually we need to represent this as reversed piece
                    result[-1] = (j+1, i+1)  # x > y means reversed
                    remaining = ""
                    found = True
                    break
            if found:
                break
                
        if found:
            break
            
        # Try to find a smaller substring that matches part of remaining
        # This is getting complex - let's use a different approach
        
        # Try all possible lengths from 1 to len(remaining)
        # and see if there's a substring that matches either forward or backward
        found = False
        for length in range(1, len(remaining) + 1):
            for i in range(len(s) - length + 1):
                substr = s[i:i+length]
                # Try match forward
                if substr == remaining[:length]:
                    # This could be our piece
                    result.append((i+1, i+length))
                    remaining = remaining[length:]
                    found = True
                    break
                # Try match backward
                if substr[::-1] == remaining[:length]:
                    result.append((i+length+1, i+1))  # reversed: x > y
                    remaining = remaining[length:]
                    found = True
                    break
            if found:
                break
                
        if not found:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    # Output result
    print(len(result))
    for x, y in result:
        print(x, y)

solve()
```

Actually, let me implement a more systematic solution:

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    t = input().strip()
    
    # Check if any character in t is not in s
    s_set = set(s)
    for char in t:
        if char not in s_set:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    # We'll use dynamic programming or greedy with backtracking
    # But given constraints, let's try greedy with potential optimization
    
    # Create a set of all possible substrings of s
    substrings = set()
    for i in range(len(s)):
        for j in range(i, len(s)):
            substrings.add(s[i:j+1])
            substrings.add(s[i:j+1][::-1])
    
    # Now build t piece by piece greedily
    result = []
    i = 0
    
    while i < len(t):
        # Try to find the longest possible match from current position
        found = False
        
        # Try to match with forward substring
        for j in range(i + 1, len(t) + 1):
            target = t[i:j]
            if target in substrings:
                # Find where this substring appears in s
                for pos in range(len(s) - len(target) + 1):
                    if s[pos:pos+len(target)] == target:
                        result.append((pos + 1, pos + len(target)))
                        i = j
                        found = True
                        break
                if found:
                    break
            # Try reverse
            rev_target = target[::-1]
            if rev_target in substrings:
                for pos in range(len(s) - len(target) + 1):
                    if s[pos:pos+len(target)] == rev_target:
                        result.append((pos + len(target) + 1, pos + 1))  # reversed
                        i = j
                        found = True
                        break
                if found:
                    break
        
        if not found:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    print(len(result))
    for x, y in result:
        print(x, y)
```

Actually, I see a problem - the approach above might not find valid positions since we need to ensure we can extract substrings from s. Let me simplify and fix:

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    t = input().strip()
    
    # Check if any character in t is not in s
    s_set = set(s)
    for char in t:
        if char not in s_set:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    # Build result by trying to match prefixes of t
    result = []
    i = 0
    
    while i < len(t):
        # Try all possible lengths from 1 to remaining length
        found = False
        remaining_len = len(t) - i
        
        for length in range(1, remaining_len + 1):
            target = t[i:i+length]
            
            # Check if target appears as substring in s (forward)
            found_forward = False
            for start in range(len(s) - length + 1):
                if s[start:start+length] == target:
                    result.append((start + 1, start + length))
                    i += length
                    found_forward = True
                    break
            
            # Check if reverse of target appears in s (as forward)
            rev_target = target[::-1]
            found_reverse = False
            for start in range(len(s) - length + 1):
                if s[start:start+length] == rev_target:
                    result.append((start + length + 1, start + 1))  # reversed because x>y
                    i += length
                    found_reverse = True
                    break
            
            if found_forward or found_reverse:
                found = True
                break
        
        if not found:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    print(len(result))
    for x, y in result:
        print(x, y)
```

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

A boy named Ayrat lives on planet AMI-1511. Each inhabitant of this planet has a talent. Specifically, Ayrat loves running, moreover, just running is not enough for him. He is dreaming of making running a real art.

First, he wants to construct the running track with coating t. On planet AMI-1511 the coating of the track is the sequence of colored blocks, where each block is denoted as the small English letter. Therefore, every coating can be treated as a string.

Unfortunately, blocks aren't freely sold to non-business customers, but Ayrat found an infinite number of coatings s. Also, he has scissors and glue. Ayrat is going to buy some coatings s, then cut out from each of them exactly one continuous piece (substring) and glue it to the end of his track coating. Moreover, he may choose to flip this block before glueing it. Ayrat want's to know the minimum number of coating s he needs to buy in order to get the coating t for his running track. Of course, he also want's to know some way to achieve the answer.


-----Input-----

First line of the input contains the string s — the coating that is present in the shop. Second line contains the string t — the coating Ayrat wants to obtain. Both strings are non-empty, consist of only small English letters and their length doesn't exceed 2100.


-----Output-----

The first line should contain the minimum needed number of coatings n or -1 if it's impossible to create the desired coating.

If the answer is not -1, then the following n lines should contain two integers x_{i} and y_{i} — numbers of ending blocks in the corresponding piece. If x_{i} ≤ y_{i} then this piece is used in the regular order, and if x_{i} > y_{i} piece is used in the reversed order. Print the pieces in the order they should be glued to get the string t.


-----Examples-----
Input
abc
cbaabc

Output
2
3 1
1 3

Input
aaabrytaaa
ayrat

Output
3
1 1
6 5
8 7

Input
ami
no

Output
-1

In the first sample string "cbaabc" = "cba" + "abc".

In the second sample: "ayrat" = "a" + "yr" + "at".

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have an infinite supply of coating string `s`
2. We can cut out any substring from `s` and either use it as-is or in reverse
3. We want to build target string `t` by concatenating these substrings
4. We need to find the minimum number of substrings needed

## Key Insights

1. If `t` contains a character not present in `s`, it's impossible → return -1
2. We need to decompose `t` into substrings that are either:
   - Direct substrings of `s`, or  
   - Reverse substrings of `s`
3. We want the minimum number of such substrings

## Algorithm Approach

This is essentially a dynamic programming problem where we want to minimize the number of pieces.

1. First check if all characters in `t` exist in `s` - if not, return -1
2. Use dynamic programming to find the minimum number of pieces needed
3. For each position in `t`, try all possible substrings ending at that position that are valid (either in `s` or reversed in `s`)
4. Keep track of the path (which pieces to use)

## Optimization Considerations

Since strings can be up to 2100 characters, we need an efficient approach. We can:
- Precompute all possible substrings of `s` and their reverses
- Use DP where `dp[i]` = minimum number of pieces to form `t[0:i]`
- For each position, try all possible substrings ending at that position

## Step-by-step Solution

1. Check if all characters in `t` exist in `s`
2. Use DP to build the minimum solution
3. Backtrack to reconstruct the pieces

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    t = input().strip()
    
    # Check if all characters in t exist in s
    s_chars = set(s)
    for char in t:
        if char not in s_chars:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    n = len(t)
    if n == 0:
        print(0)
        return
    
    # Precompute all possible substrings of s and their reverses
    # We'll store valid substrings and their positions in s
    valid_substrings = {}
    
    for i in range(len(s)):
        for j in range(i, len(s)):
            substr = s[i:j+1]
            reverse_substr = s[i:j+1][::-1]
            valid_substrings[substr] = True
            valid_substrings[reverse_substr] = True
    
    # DP approach
    # dp[i] = minimum number of pieces to form t[0:i]
    # prev[i] = (last piece start, last piece end, piece type) 
    # where type: 0 = original, 1 = reversed
    
    dp = [float('inf')] * (n + 1)
    prev = [None] * (n + 1)
    dp[0] = 0
    
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        # Try all possible ending positions for the last piece
        for j in range(i):
            if dp[j] == float('inf'):
                continue
                
            # Try all possible substrings that could end at position i-1
            # We need a substring from position j to i-1 in t
            substr_to_match = t[j:i]
            
            # Check if this substring is in our valid substrings
            if substr_to_match in valid_substrings:
                if dp[j] + 1 < dp[i]:
                    dp[i] = dp[j] + 1
                    prev[i] = (j, i-1, 0)  # original order
            
            # Check if reverse of this substring is valid
            reverse_substr = substr_to_match[::-1]
            if reverse_substr in valid_substrings:
                if dp[j] + 1 < dp[i]:
                    dp[i] = dp[j] + 1
                    prev[i] = (j, i-1, 1)  # reversed order
    
    if dp[n] == float('inf'):
        print(-1)
        return
    
    # Reconstruct the solution
    result = []
    pos = n
    while pos > 0:
        if prev[pos] is None:
            break
            
        j, k, is_reversed = prev[pos]
        # The piece covers from j to k in t
        # In the original string s, this piece corresponds to a substring
        piece = t[j:k+1]
        
        if is_reversed == 0:
            # Original order - find start and end indices in s
            # We need to find the first occurrence of piece in s
            start_pos = -1
            for i in range(len(s) - len(piece) + 1):
                if s[i:i+len(piece)] == piece:
                    start_pos = i
                    break
            result.append((start_pos + 1, len(piece)))  # 1-indexed
        else:
            # Reversed order - piece in s is the reverse of what we see
            # So we need the reverse of piece in s
            reverse_piece = piece[::-1]
            start_pos = -1
            for i in range(len(s) - len(piece) + 1):
                if s[i:i+len(piece)] == reverse_piece:
                    start_pos = i
                    break
            result.append((start_pos + 1, len(piece)))  # 1-indexed
        
        pos = j
    
    # Reverse the result to get correct order
    result.reverse()
    
    print(dp[n])
    for start, length in result:
        # Convert to (x, y) where x <= y for original, x > y for reversed
        # For the piece from start to start+length-1 in s
        end = start + length - 1
        if start <= end:
            print(start, end)
        else:
            print(end, start)  # This should not happen since we're using valid substrings

solve()
```

The pieces don't have to be contiguous in s - we can pick any substring from s. But we need to know exactly which positions to pick.

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    t = input().strip()
    
    # Check if all characters in t exist in s
    s_chars = set(s)
    for char in t:
        if char not in s_chars:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    n = len(t)
    if n == 0:
        print(0)
        return
    
    # DP approach
    # dp[i] = minimum number of pieces to form t[0:i]
    # prev[i] = (start_pos_in_t, end_pos_in_t, reverse_flag) 
    # where reverse_flag: 0 = original, 1 = reversed
    
    dp = [float('inf')] * (n + 1)
    prev = [None] * (n + 1)
    dp[0] = 0
    
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        # Try all possible previous positions
        for j in range(i):
            if dp[j] == float('inf'):
                continue
                
            piece = t[j:i]
            
            # Check if piece is a substring of s
            if piece in s:
                if dp[j] + 1 < dp[i]:
                    dp[i] = dp[j] + 1
                    prev[i] = (j, i-1, 0)
            
            # Check if reverse of piece is a substring of s
            rev_piece = piece[::-1]
            if rev_piece in s:
                if dp[j] + 1 < dp[i]:
                    dp[i] = dp[j] + 1
                    prev[i] = (j, i-1, 1)
    
    if dp[n] == float('inf'):
        print(-1)
        return
    
    # Reconstruct the solution
    result = []
    pos = n
    while pos > 0:
        j, k, is_reversed = prev[pos]
        
        piece = t[j:pos]
        
        if is_reversed == 0:
            # Original order - find first occurrence of piece in s
            start_pos = -1
            for i in range(len(s) - len(piece) + 1):
                if s[i:i+len(piece)] == piece:
                    start_pos = i
                    break
            result.append((start_pos + 1, start_pos + len(piece)))  # 1-indexed
        else:
            # Reversed order - find first occurrence of reverse piece in s
            rev_piece = piece[::-1]
            start_pos = -1
            for i in range(len(s) - len(piece) + 1):
                if s[i:i+len(piece)] == rev_piece:
                    start_pos = i
                    break
            result.append((start_pos + 1, start_pos + len(piece)))  # 1-indexed
        
        pos = j
    
    # Reverse to get correct order
    result.reverse()
    
    print(dp[n])
    for start, end in result:
        print(start, end)
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have an infinite supply of a string `s` that we can cut out substrings from
2. We can cut out any continuous substring from `s` and either use it in original order or reversed order
3. We want to build target string `t` by concatenating these substrings
4. We need to find the minimum number of such substrings needed

## Key Insights

1. If `t` contains any character not present in `s`, it's impossible → return -1
2. We want to build `t` from left to right, trying to match as much of `t` as possible with existing substrings from `s`
3. For each position in `t`, we need to find a substring in `s` that can match the remaining portion of `t` (either directly or in reverse)
4. We need to minimize the total number of substrings used

## Algorithm Approach

1. First check if all characters in `t` exist in `s`. If not, return -1.
2. Use a greedy approach: go through `t` from left to right, and at each position, try to find the longest possible substring from `s` that matches the current suffix of `t`.
3. For each segment, we can either use it as is or reverse it.

However, there's a crucial insight: since we have infinite copies of `s`, we can find any substring that appears in `s` (or its reverse) and use it.

## Detailed Algorithm

1. Check if every character in `t` exists in `s`. If not, return -1.
2. For each position in `t`, we need to find a substring of `s` that matches the remaining part of `t`.
3. We can use dynamic programming or greedy approach to minimize the number of pieces.

Actually, let me think of a simpler greedy approach:
- We traverse `t` from left to right
- At each step, we look for a substring in `s` that matches the current suffix of `t` (either directly or in reverse)
- We want to maximize the length of each piece to minimize the total count

## Implementation Strategy

1. Check if all characters of `t` are in `s`
2. Use a greedy matching: from left to right of `t`, find the longest possible match from `s` (or reverse) that can be used
3. For each match, store the start and end indices in `s` (1-indexed)

Let me implement this:

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    t = input().strip()
    
    # Check if all characters in t exist in s
    s_set = set(s)
    for char in t:
        if char not in s_set:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    # Find all possible substrings of s and their reverses
    # We want to match t from left to right
    result = []
    i = 0  # current position in t
    
    while i < len(t):
        # Try to find a substring of s that matches t[i:]
        # Try both original and reversed
        found = False
        
        # Try to match with original substring
        for start in range(len(s)):
            for end in range(start, len(s)):
                substring = s[start:end+1]
                if i + len(substring) <= len(t) and t[i:i+len(substring)] == substring:
                    # Found a match in original order
                    result.append((start + 1, end + 1))  # 1-indexed
                    i += len(substring)
                    found = True
                    break
            if found:
                break
                
        if not found:
            # Try to match with reversed substring
            for start in range(len(s)):
                for end in range(start, len(s)):
                    reversed_substr = s[start:end+1][::-1]
                    if i + len(reversed_substr) <= len(t) and t[i:i+len(reversed_substr)] == reversed_substr:
                        result.append((start + 1, end + 1))  # 1-indexed, reversed
                        i += len(reversed_substr)
                        found = True
                        break
                if found:
                    break
        
        if not found:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    print(len(result))
    for x, y in result:
        print(x, y)
```

## Optimized Approach

The key insight is that we need to check if we can form `t` by using pieces from `s` (original or reversed). Since we want to minimize the number of pieces, we should use as long pieces as possible.

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    t = input().strip()
    
    # Check if all characters in t exist in s
    s_set = set(s)
    for char in t:
        if char not in s_set:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    # Precompute all possible substrings and their reversed versions
    # For efficiency, we'll just generate them as needed
    result = []
    i = 0
    
    while i < len(t):
        found = False
        
        # Try to find the longest match from current position
        for length in range(len(s), 0, -1):
            # Try original substring
            substrings_original = []
            for start in range(len(s) - length + 1):
                substr = s[start:start + length]
                if t[i:i + length] == substr:
                    result.append((start + 1, start + length))
                    i += length
                    found = True
                    break
                    
            if found:
                break
                
            # Try reversed substring
            for start in range(len(s) - length + 1):
                reversed_substr = s[start:start + length][::-1]
                if t[i:i + length] == reversed_substr:
                    result.append((start + 1, start + length))
                    i += length
                    found = True
                    break
                    
            if found:
                break
        
        if not found:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    print(len(result))
    for x, y in result:
        print(x, y)
```

Actually, this might still be too slow. Let me think about it differently.

The issue is that for each position, we're checking all possible lengths from longest to shortest, and for each length we're checking all possible starting positions. This is O(n^3) in worst case, which might be borderline for 2100.

But let me check the constraints: length ≤ 2100, and we're doing multiple checks. The outer loop is at most 2100 iterations, and for each we do O(n^2) work, so total O(n^3) which is 2100^3 = about 9.3e9 operations - too slow.

## Better Approach

We can precompute all substrings and their reverses, store them in a dictionary with the pattern as key.

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    t = input().strip()
    
    # Check if all characters in t exist in s
    s_set = set(s)
    for char in t:
        if char not in s_set:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    # Precompute all possible substrings and their reverses
    # Map from pattern to (start, end) pairs
    pattern_to_segments = {}
    
    # Add original substrings
    for i in range(len(s)):
        for j in range(i, len(s)):
            substr = s[i:j+1]
            if substr not in pattern_to_segments:
                pattern_to_segments[substr] = []
            pattern_to_segments[substr].append((i+1, j+1))
    
    # Add reversed substrings
    for i in range(len(s)):
        for j in range(i, len(s)):
            reversed_substr = s[i:j+1][::-1]
            if reversed_substr not in pattern_to_segments:
                pattern_to_segments[reversed_substr] = []
            pattern_to_segments[reversed_substr].append((i+1, j+1))
    
    result = []
    i = 0
    
    while i < len(t):
        # Find the longest possible match
        found = False
        max_length = 0
        
        for length in range(len(t) - i, 0, -1):
            substr = t[i:i+length]
            if substr in pattern_to_segments:
                # Found a match, use the first segment
                start, end = pattern_to_segments[substr][0]
                result.append((start, end))
                i += length
                found = True
                break
                
        if not found:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    print(len(result))
    for x, y in result:
        print(x, y)

solve()
```

This is still O(n^3) in worst case, but let's optimize further by not generating all substrings.

Actually, since the strings can be long, I'll stick with the simpler greedy approach with reasonable optimization.

```python
s = input().strip()
t = input().strip()

# Check if all characters in t exist in s
s_set = set(s)
for char in t:
    if char not in s_set:
        print(-1)
        exit()

result = []
i = 0

while i < len(t):
    found = False
    # Try decreasing lengths from longest possible to shortest
    for length in range(min(len(t) - i, len(s)), 0, -1):
        # Check if t[i:i+length] appears as a substring in s (original)
        found_original = False
        for start in range(len(s) - length + 1):
            if s[start:start+length] == t[i:i+length]:
                result.append((start + 1, start + length))
                i += length
                found = True
                found_original = True
                break
                
        if found:
            break
            
        # Check if t[i:i+length] appears as a reversed substring in s
        found_reversed = False
        for start in range(len(s) - length + 1):
            reversed_substr = s[start:start+length][::-1]
            if reversed_substr == t[i:i+length]:
                result.append((start + 1, start + length))
                i += length
                found = True
                found_reversed = True
                break
                
        if found:
            break
    
    if not found:
        print(-1)
        exit()

print(len(result))
for x, y in result:
    print(x, y)
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have an infinite supply of string `s` (the coating available in shop)
2. We need to create target string `t` by taking substrings from `s` and either using them as-is or reversing them
3. We want to minimize the number of such substrings we need to buy
4. Each substring can be taken from any position in `s` (it's infinite, so we can take from any position in the repeated pattern)

## Key Insights

1. If `t` contains a character not present in `s`, it's impossible → return -1
2. We need to find the minimum number of substrings from `s` that, when properly ordered and possibly reversed, form `t`
3. This is essentially a dynamic programming problem where we build `t` character by character
4. For each position in `t`, we need to find a substring from `s` that matches either the current segment of `t` or its reverse

## Approach

1. First check if all characters in `t` exist in `s`. If not, return -1.
2. Use dynamic programming where `dp[i]` represents the minimum number of pieces needed to form the prefix `t[0:i]`
3. For each position, try all possible substrings from `s` that match a suffix of the current target string (either directly or reversed)
4. Track the exact cuts and their positions

Wait, let me reconsider. This is more complex. Let me think about it differently.

Actually, since we have infinite copies of `s`, we can take any substring from `s` at any position. So for each position in `t`, we need to find a substring in `s` that can match a portion of `t`.

The key insight is that for each segment of `t`, we need to find whether it matches a substring of `s` or the reverse of a substring of `s`.

## Algorithm

1. Check if all characters in `t` are in `s`. If not, return -1.
2. Use dynamic programming to find minimum segments:
   - `dp[i]` = minimum number of pieces to form `t[0:i]`
   - `prev[i]` = position where we last completed a segment
3. For each position, try all possible lengths of the last piece and check if that piece (in original or reversed) matches the corresponding part of `t`.

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    t = input().strip()
    
    # Check if all characters in t exist in s
    s_chars = set(s)
    for char in t:
        if char not in s_chars:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    n = len(t)
    # dp[i] = minimum number of pieces to form t[0:i]
    dp = [float('inf')] * (n + 1)
    dp[0] = 0
    
    # prev[i] stores the ending position of the previous piece
    prev = [-1] * (n + 1)
    
    # For each position in t
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        # Try all possible lengths of the current piece
        for j in range(i):
            # The current piece covers from j to i-1 in t
            piece = t[j:i]
            
            # Check if this piece matches any substring of s (original or reversed)
            found = False
            for start in range(len(s)):
                # Check original substring
                substr = s[start:start + len(piece)]
                if substr == piece:
                    # We can use this piece from s starting at position start
                    if dp[j] + 1 < dp[i]:
                        dp[i] = dp[j] + 1
                        prev[i] = j
                        found = True
                        break
                
                # Check reversed substring
                reversed_piece = piece[::-1]
                reversed_substr = s[start:start + len(reversed_piece)]
                if reversed_substr == reversed_piece:
                    # We can use the reversed piece
                    if dp[j] + 1 < dp[i]:
                        dp[i] = dp[j] + 1
                        prev[i] = j
                        found = True
                        break
            
            # If we didn't find a match for this piece length, continue to next length
            # (we already have found at least one if we broke out)
        
        # If we couldn't find a valid piece for position i, we need to continue
        # But our loop above should have found something
    
    # If we couldn't reach the end, it's impossible
    if dp[n] == float('inf'):
        print(-1)
        return
    
    # Now reconstruct the solution
    result = []
    pos = n
    while pos > 0:
        start = prev[pos]
        piece = t[start:pos]
        # Find the actual start position in s for this piece
        found = False
        for start_s in range(len(s)):
            # Check original
            substr = s[start_s:start_s + len(piece)]
            if substr == piece:
                result.append((start_s + 1, start_s + len(piece)))  # 1-indexed
                found = True
                break
            
            # Check reversed
            reversed_piece = piece[::-1]
            reversed_substr = s[start_s:start_s + len(reversed_piece)]
            if reversed_substr == reversed_piece:
                result.append((start_s + 1, start_s + len(reversed_piece)))
                found = True
                break
        
        if not found:
            # This should not happen if dp[n] is valid
            break
        
        pos = start
    
    # Reverse the result since we built it backwards
    result.reverse()
    
    print(dp[n])
    for x, y in result:
        print(x, y)

solve()
```

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    t = input().strip()
    
    # Check if all characters in t exist in s
    s_chars = set(s)
    for char in t:
        if char not in s_chars:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    n = len(t)
    
    # dp[i] = minimum number of pieces to form t[0:i]
    dp = [float('inf')] * (n + 1)
    dp[0] = 0
    
    # parent[i] stores which position we came from
    parent = [-1] * (n + 1)
    
    # For each position in t
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        # Try all possible previous positions
        for j in range(i):
            piece = t[j:i]
            piece_len = len(piece)
            
            # Check if piece appears in s (original)
            found_original = False
            for start in range(len(s) - piece_len + 1):
                if s[start:start + piece_len] == piece:
                    if dp[j] + 1 < dp[i]:
                        dp[i] = dp[j] + 1
                        parent[i] = j
                    found_original = True
            
            # Check if piece reversed appears in s (original)
            reversed_piece = piece[::-1]
            found_reversed = False
            for start in range(len(s) - piece_len + 1):
                if s[start:start + piece_len] == reversed_piece:
                    if dp[j] + 1 < dp[i]:
                        dp[i] = dp[j] + 1
                        parent[i] = j
                    found_reversed = True
            
            # If we found a valid match, update dp[i]
            # Note: we don't need to break since we want minimum pieces
    
    if dp[n] == float('inf'):
        print(-1)
        return
    
    # Reconstruct the solution
    result = []
    pos = n
    while pos > 0:
        prev_pos = parent[pos]
        piece = t[prev_pos:pos]
        piece_len = len(piece)
        
        # Find the actual start position in s for this piece
        # Try original first
        found = False
        for start in range(len(s) - piece_len + 1):
            if s[start:start + piece_len] == piece:
                result.append((start + 1, start + piece_len))
                found = True
                break
        
        if not found:
            # Try reversed
            reversed_piece = piece[::-1]
            for start in range(len(s) - piece_len + 1):
                if s[start:start + piece_len] == reversed_piece:
                    result.append((start + 1, start + piece_len))
                    found = True
                    break
        
        pos = prev_pos
    
    result.reverse()
    
    print(dp[n])
    for x, y in result:
        print(x, y)
```

### Answer: (use the provided format with backticks)

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have an infinite supply of a base string `s`
2. We can cut out any substring from `s` (continuous piece) and either use it in original order or reversed order
3. We want to build target string `t` using the minimum number of such operations
4. We need to output the starting and ending positions of each substring used, with the condition that if x ≤ y, we use original order; if x > y, we use reversed order

## Key Insights

1. If `t` contains a character not present in `s`, it's impossible → return -1
2. We need to break down `t` into segments that can be found in `s` (either directly or as reverses)
3. For each segment, we need to find positions in `s` to extract it
4. We want to minimize the number of segments

## Approach

1. First check if all characters in `t` are present in `s`. If not, return -1.
2. Use dynamic programming or greedy approach to find the minimum number of segments.
3. For each position in `t`, try to extend the current segment as long as possible by checking if the substring exists in `s` or its reverse.
4. For each possible segment, find the lexicographically smallest valid position in `s` that matches.

However, since the strings can be up to 2100 characters, we need an efficient approach.

## Optimization Strategy

1. Precompute all substrings of `s` and their reverse
2. For each position in `t`, try to match a prefix of `t` with a valid substring from `s` or its reverse
3. Use greedy approach: match as long as possible at each step

## Step-by-step Solution

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    t = input().strip()
    
    # Check if all characters in t are present in s
    s_set = set(s)
    t_set = set(t)
    if not t_set.issubset(s_set):
        print(-1)
        return
    
    # Precompute all substrings of s and their reverse positions
    # For each substring in s, store all its positions (i, j) such that s[i:j+1] exists
    substrings = {}
    n = len(s)
    
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i, n):
            substr = s[i:j+1]
            reverse_substr = s[i:j+1][::-1]
            
            # Store positions for original and reversed versions
            if substr not in substrings:
                substrings[substr] = []
            if reverse_substr not in substrings:
                substrings[reverse_substr] = []
            
            # Add original substring
            substrings[substr].append((i, j))
            # Add reversed substring  
            substrings[reverse_substr].append((j, i))  # reversed positions
    
    # Greedy approach: try to match t by finding longest possible segments
    result = []
    pos = 0
    
    while pos < len(t):
        found = False
        
        # Try to extend from current position
        for length in range(1, len(t) - pos + 1):
            segment = t[pos:pos + length]
            
            # Check if segment exists in s (original or reversed)
            if segment in substrings:
                # Find the earliest available segment
                for start, end in substrings[segment]:
                    # Validate that this segment appears in s
                    if start <= end and start >= 0 and end < len(s):
                        result.append((start + 1, end + 1))  # Convert to 1-indexed
                        pos += length
                        found = True
                        break
            elif segment[::-1] in substrings:
                # Check if reverse exists
                for start, end in substrings[segment[::-1]]:
                    if start <= end and start >= 0 and end < len(s):
                        # This is the reverse of the segment
                        result.append((end + 1, start + 1))  # reversed order
                        pos += length
                        found = True
                        break
        
        if not found:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    # Output result
    print(len(result))
    for start, end in result:
        print(start, end)

solve()
```

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    t = input().strip()
    
    # Check if all characters in t are present in s
    s_set = set(s)
    t_set = set(t)
    if not t_set.issubset(s_set):
        print(-1)
        return
    
    # Build a dictionary of all possible substrings with their positions
    substring_positions = {}
    
    n = len(s)
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i, n):
            substr = s[i:j+1]
            rev_substr = substr[::-1]
            
            if substr not in substring_positions:
                substring_positions[substr] = []
            if rev_substr not in substring_positions:
                substring_positions[rev_substr] = []
            
            substring_positions[substr].append((i, j))  # original
            substring_positions[rev_substr].append((j, i))  # reversed
    
    # Greedy matching: try to match as long as possible
    result = []
    idx = 0
    
    while idx < len(t):
        matched = False
        
        # Try all possible lengths from 1 to remaining
        for length in range(1, len(t) - idx + 1):
            segment = t[idx:idx + length]
            
            # Check if original segment exists in s
            if segment in substring_positions:
                # Find first occurrence (smallest start position)
                positions = substring_positions[segment]
                if positions:
                    start, end = min(positions, key=lambda x: x[0])
                    result.append((start + 1, end + 1))  # 1-indexed
                    idx += length
                    matched = True
                    break
            
            # Check if reversed segment exists
            rev_segment = segment[::-1]
            if rev_segment in substring_positions:
                positions = substring_positions[rev_segment]
                if positions:
                    start, end = min(positions, key=lambda x: x[0])
                    result.append((end + 1, start + 1))  # reversed, 1-indexed
                    idx += length
                    matched = True
                    break
        
        if not matched:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    print(len(result))
    for start, end in result:
        print(start, end)
```

You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

You are now examining a unique method to sort a sequence of numbers in increasing order. The method only allows swapping of two numbers that have a common prime factor. For example, a sequence [6, 4, 2, 3, 7] can be sorted using the following steps.
Step 0: 6 4 2 3 7 (given sequence)
Step 1: 2 4 6 3 7 (elements 6 and 2 swapped)
Step 2: 2 6 4 3 7 (elements 4 and 6 swapped)
Step 3: 2 3 4 6 7 (elements 6 and 3 swapped)


Depending on the nature of the sequence, however, this approach may fail to complete the sorting. You have given a name "Coprime sort" to this approach and are now examining if a given sequence is coprime-sortable.

Make a program to determine if a given sequence can be sorted in increasing order by iterating an arbitrary number of swapping operations of two elements that have a common prime number.



Input

The input is given in the following format.


$N$
$a_1$ $a_2$ $...$ $a_N$


The first line provides the number of elements included in the sequence $N$ ($2 \leq N \leq 10^5$). The second line provides an array of integers $a_i$ ($2 \leq a_i \leq 10^5$) that constitute the sequence.

Output

Output "1" if the sequence is coprime-sortable in increasing order, or "0" otherwise.

Examples

Input

5
6 4 2 3 7


Output

1


Input

7
2 9 6 5 6 7 3


Output

0

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to determine if a sequence can be sorted in increasing order by swapping elements that have a common prime factor
2. Two elements can be swapped if they share at least one prime factor
3. We need to output "1" if coprime-sortable, "0" otherwise

## Key Insights

1. If two numbers share a common prime factor, we can swap them
2. This means numbers that share prime factors can be rearranged among themselves
3. The question becomes: can we group numbers by their prime factors and check if within each group, the numbers can be rearranged to their correct positions?

## Core Concept

This is essentially about connected components in a graph where:
- Each number is a node
- Two numbers are connected if they share a prime factor
- Within each connected component, we can rearrange elements freely

For the sequence to be coprime-sortable:
- For each connected component, the relative order of elements within that component must be consistent with the sorted order
- That is, if we look at the positions of elements in the original sequence and in the sorted sequence, and group them by their prime factor connections, we should not have any conflicts

## Algorithm

1. Find all prime factors for each number in the sequence
2. Build a graph where numbers are connected if they share prime factors
3. Find connected components
4. For each connected component, check if the relative order of elements in the original sequence and sorted sequence is consistent

Wait, let me reconsider. Actually, since we can swap elements that share prime factors, all elements in the same connected component can be freely rearranged. This means:

- Elements that belong to the same connected component can be rearranged in any order
- For the sorting to be possible, the elements that should end up in certain positions in the sorted array must be able to reach those positions through swaps

Actually, a simpler approach:
- For each prime factor, all numbers that have this prime factor form a connected component
- Within each connected component, we can rearrange elements freely
- So for the sequence to be coprime-sortable, when we look at the sorted array, for each connected component, the elements that eventually go to positions in that component should be exactly the elements of that component

But I'm overcomplicating. Let's think about it differently:

The key insight is that if two elements can be swapped, then they belong to the same "swappable group" defined by shared prime factors. 

So:
1. Group numbers by their prime factors
2. For each prime factor, all numbers with that factor are in the same group
3. These groups form connected components
4. Within each group, we can rearrange elements freely
5. For the sequence to be coprime-sortable, the elements that should be in the final sorted positions must be reachable from those positions through the connected components

Actually, the simpler approach is:
- Find all prime factors of all numbers
- Group elements by their prime factor sets
- For each group (connected component), the elements in that group can be rearranged freely
- The sequence is coprime-sortable if and only if for every position, the element that should be there in the sorted array is actually in the same group as the element currently at that position

No, that's not right either.

Let me think again:

The correct approach:
1. Build a graph where nodes are elements and edges exist between elements that share a prime factor
2. Find connected components
3. For the sequence to be coprime-sortable, each element must be able to reach its final position in the sorted array

Actually, since we can swap elements within connected components freely, the relative order of elements within each connected component can be changed arbitrarily. 

Therefore, the sequence is coprime-sortable if and only if for each connected component, the multiset of elements in that component in the original array is the same as the multiset of elements in that component in the sorted array.

This is the key insight!

## Step-by-step Solution

1. Find prime factors of each number
2. Group elements by prime factors (build connected components)
3. For each connected component, check if the multiset of original elements equals the multiset of sorted elements in that component

```python
def get_prime_factors(n):
    factors = set()
    d = 2
    while d * d <= n:
        while n % d == 0:
            factors.add(d)
            n //= d
        d += 1
    if n > 1:
        factors.add(n)
    return factors

def solve():
    n = int(input())
    arr = list(map(int, input().split()))
    
    # Get sorted array
    sorted_arr = sorted(arr)
    
    # Find prime factors for each element
    prime_factors = []
    for num in arr:
        prime_factors.append(get_prime_factors(num))
    
    # Build connected components using union-find or by grouping
    # Group elements by their prime factors - elements that share any prime factor are connected
    
    # For each element, we'll find all prime factors it has
    # Two elements are connected if they share at least one prime factor
    
    # We can group by the union of prime factors
    # For each element, create a "signature" of its prime factors
    # Elements with overlapping prime factors can be swapped
    
    # Instead, let's create groups based on prime factors
    # For each prime factor, all elements having that factor are in the same group
    
    # Find all primes that appear in any number
    all_primes = set()
    for factors in prime_factors:
        all_primes.update(factors)
    
    # Group elements by which primes they have
    elements_groups = {}  # key: prime factor, value: list of indices that have this prime
    
    for prime in all_primes:
        elements_groups[prime] = []
    
    # Assign each element to which primes it has
    for i, factors in enumerate(prime_factors):
        for prime in factors:
            elements_groups[prime].append(i)
    
    # Now we have multiple groups (one for each prime factor)
    # Elements in the same group can be freely rearranged
    
    # For each prime factor group, check if the multiset of elements in original array 
    # matches the multiset of elements in sorted array for that group
    
    # But we need to find connected components - actually, if two elements share any prime factor, 
    # they can be swapped, so all elements sharing at least one prime factor form a connected component
    
    # Let's build connected components using union-find
    parent = list(range(n))
    
    def find(x):
        if parent[x] != x:
            parent[x] = find(parent[x])
        return parent[x]
    
    def union(x, y):
        px, py = find(x), find(y)
        if px != py:
            parent[px] = py
    
    # Connect elements that share prime factors
    # For each prime factor, connect all elements that have it
    for prime, indices in elements_groups.items():
        for i in range(1, len(indices)):
            union(indices[0], indices[i])
    
    # Now group elements by their connected component
    comp_group = {}
    for i in range(n):
        root = find(i)
        if root not in comp_group:
            comp_group[root] = []
        comp_group[root].append(i)
    
    # For each connected component, check if the multiset of original elements 
    # matches the multiset of sorted elements
    for component in comp_group.values():
        # Get elements in original array for this component
        original_elements = [arr[i] for i in component]
        # Get elements in sorted array for this component
        sorted_elements = [sorted_arr[i] for i in component]
        
        # Check if multisets are equal
        if sorted(original_elements) != sorted(sorted_elements):
            return 0
    
    return 1

print(solve())
```
